## "Original Sin" Notebook Original Sin (Arnauld)

Jonathan EdwardsNotebook on Original Sin

These notes are part of Edwards' apparatus for drafting his treatise defending the doctrine of original sin. As early as his epistle to the Northampton congregation of c. June 1752 appended to *Misrepresentations Corrected*, Edwards had lamented the influence of John Taylor's "scheme of religion," including his views on "the whole doctrine of original sin," in *The Scripture-Doctrine of Original Sin, Proposed to Free and Candid Examination* (London, 1740).[^001-note1] But in a letter to Thomas Foxcroft of Feb. 11, 1757, Edwards writes that he had "lately been writing a defense of the doctrine of original sin; wherein I have largely handled the principal arguments I know of for that doctrine; and have particularly considered everything, of any consequence, in Dr. Taylor's book against that doctrine; a book that has done more to root out the gospel, in all this western part of New England, than any other book. I have almost prepared this for the press."[^001-note2] The notes preserved here in the first part correlate to Ch. III, sec. 2, "Observations on Romans 3:9-24," discussing the nature of "collective bodies" and justification. Entries towards the end, including a draft of a printing proposal, language for the preface, and even a "motto" for the treatise on original sin, reflect Edwards' initial plan of publishing his views on original sin and true virtue in the same work.

*****

The cover of the MS notebook (BRBL, f. 1241) is made of a portion of the *New York Mercury* for April 26, 1756; the front cover is one piece, the back a large piece doubled over. The MS itself is composed of a disbound and mostly re-stitched copy of Antoine Arnauld's *De la Frequente Communion* (Paris, 1643), pp. 105-10 (loose) and pp. 145-256 (stitched), 53 leaves total. To utilize the printed pages, Edwards turned them upside down and wrote in the margins. The extant pages numbered by Edwards are pp. 35-40, 65-66, and 79-100, with the remaining ones unnumbered. His notes extend through MS p. 80.

Edwards received a copy of Arnauld's work from John Erskine in late June 1752. In thanking Erskine, he commented: "Arnauld On Frequent Communion will not be very profitable to me, by reason of my not understanding the French." It is possible these notes may have been started sometime between 1752 and 1756, since Edwards may have stitched on the cover after he began them. In all likelihood, however, the cover provides a terminus a quo for the notes.

THAT RECONCILED and RECONCILIATION in this Place (Romans 5:10-11) is to be understood in this, general, collective Sense, will appear, if we consider; that thus it best corresponds to the *Justification* (the FIRST *Justification*) the Apostle is arguing for, in this Part of the Epistle." Pp. 340-41, on Romans 3:10-19: "The Apostle is here speaking of Bodies of People, or of *Jews* and *Gentiles* in a collective Capacity. . . . In fine, we cannot have a just Idea of the Apostle's Arguments, unless we keep in Mind, that he is arguing concerning the Rejection of *Jews*, and Reception of *Gentiles*, in a General, collective Capacity, to the *present* Privileges of the Church and Covenant of God; namely, in such a Sense and Capacity, that some good and righteous Men might be left out among the *Rejected*, and some unrighteous Persons taken in among the *Elect* and *Justified*." P. 424, on Romans 9:30-31,33, on the Apostle arguing that Jews were rejected as God's people and Gentiles admitted: "This demonstrates, that he doth not mean that Justification, which puts particular Persons into the state of eteranl Salvation; but that Justification, which gives them the present Blessings, Privileges, Means and Honours of God's Church and Kingdom. For all the believing *Gentiles*, in a Body, attained to this Justification; but no body will suppose, that they all, in a Body, attained to eternal Salvation." P. 438a, on Romans 9:33: "Therefore, as this *obtaining Mercy* is no other than the *Election* and *Justification*, about which he argues in this Epistle, it is certain he doth not thereby mean the *Election* and *Justification* only of particular Persons; and so as to ascertian this everlasting Salvation: But he means such an *Elections* and *Justification* as may also be applied to Bodies of Men, with respect to their being taken into the Kingdom of God, in this World."

## Fragement II OS 045-54

Jonathan EdwardsFragment II of the Treatise on *Original Sin*

Parts of two drafts of Edwards' final treatise on original sin have survived. The piece presented here contains most of Pt. I, Ch. I, Sect. VIII, on "The Native Depravity of Mankind Appears, in That There Has Been so Little Good Effect of so Manifold and Great Means, Used to Promote Virtue in the World," parallelling pages 77-95 in the first edition.[^002-note1] Here, Edward reviews the "obstinacy" of the heathen and of the Jews in their continuing unbelief and apostacy, despite the testimony of holy prophets and kings, as well as divine appearances, deliverances, and judgments, exacerbated by their resistance to the gospel.

*****

These five folio sheets (Maid of Dordt/GRcr) are so neatly written and—almost unheard of in Edwards' MSS—conscientiously punctuated as to be fugitives from a penultimate draft, or, even more rare, from the fair copy for the printer. On page 46, in the left margin, appears a notation in a different hand, "p. 81G," that refers to the corresponding place in the printed text, which suggests this was in the hands of a printer.[^002-note2] Differences between this MS and the first edition of 1758 are provided in the notes. Minor differences, such as spelled-out ampersands and punctuation, are not noted.

CHAP. I.Oppose Wickedness[^002-note3]

SECT. VIII

which pass'd between him & his Creator [-] in Paradise.

But what was the sucess of these great Means to restrain Men

from Sin, & induce them to Virtue? Did they prove sufficient? Instead

of that the World soon grew exceeding corrupt; 'till it came to

that, to use our Author's own words, That Mankind were uni-

versally debauch'd into Lust, Sensuality, Rapine & Injustice.[^002-note4]

Then God used further Means He sent Noah a Preacher of Righte-

ousness, to warn the World of the universal Destruction which would

come upon them by a Flood of Waters, if they went on in Sin. Which

Warnings he delivered with these Circumstances, tending to strike their

Minds, & command their Attention; that he immediately went about

building that vast Structure of the Ark; in which he must employ a

great Number of Hands, & probably spent all he had in the World

to save himself and his Family. Under these uncommon Means

God waited upon them 120 Years. But all to no Effect: The whole

World, for aught[^002-note5] appears, continued obstinate, and absolutely in-

corrigible: So that Nothing remain'd to be done with them, but

utterly to destroy the Inhabitants of the Earth, and to begin a new

world from that single Family that had distinguished themselves

by their Virtue; that from them might be propagated a new and

purer Race. And accordingly this was done: And the Inhabitants

of the new World of Noah's Posterity, had these new & extraordi-

nary Means to restrain Sin, & excite to Virtue, in Addition to the

Toil, Sorrow & Common Mortality, which the World had been sub-

jected to before in Consequence of Adam's Sin; that they had seen

God testifying his dreadful displeasure for Sin, in destroying the

many Millions of Mankind all at one blow, old & young, Men

Women & Children, all without Pity on any for all the dismal

shrieks & Cries which was the World was fill'd with, when they

themselves, b were so wonderfully distinguished by God's pre-

serving Goodness, that they might be a holy Seed, being delivered

from the corrupting Examples of the old World; and were all

the Offspring of a living Parent, who[se][^002-note6] pious Instructions &

Counsels they had to inforce these Things upon them, to pre-

vent sin, & engage them to their Duty. And these Inhabitants

of the new Earth must, for a long time, have before their Eyes,

many evident, & as it were fresh & striking Efects & Signs of

that universal destruction, to affect their Minds be an affecting

be a continued affecting Admonition to 'em. And besides all

this God now shorten'd the life of Man, to about one half of

what it used to be. The shortening Man's Life Dr. T. says

p. 68. "Was that the wild Range of Ambition & Lust, might

"be brought into narrower Bounds, and have less Opportunity of

"doing Mischief; And that death being still nearer to our View,

"might be a more powerful Motive to regard less the things of

"a transitory World, and to attend to the Rules of Truth & Wis-

"dom."[^002-note7]

And now let us observe the Consequence. —— These new & extraordinary

Means, in Addition to the former, are so far from proving sufficient, that

the new World degenerated, & became corrupt, by such swift degrees

that, as dr. T. Observes, Mankind in general were sunk into Idolatry, in

about

General obstinate WickednessPART. I. [^002-note8]

about 400 after the Flood, which was but about 50 years after Noah's

Death: They became so sottishly & bru wicked & brutish, as to forsake

the true God, and turn to the Worship of inanimate Creatures.

When Things were come to this dreadful Pass, God was pleased for a

Remedy, to introduce a new and wonderful Dispensation; separating a

particular Family & People from all the rest of the World, by a Series

of most astonishing Miracles, done in the open View of the World; & fix-

ing their dwelling as it were in the Midst of the Earth, between Asia,

Europe & Africa, & in the Midst of those Nations which were most

considerable and famous for Power, Knowledge & Arts; that God might,

in an extraordinary Manner, dwell amongst that People, in visible Tokens

of his Presence, manifesting himself then there there, & from thence to

the World, by a Course of great & miraculous Operations & Effects for

many Ages: that that People might be holy to God, & as a Kingdom of

Priests, and might stand as a City on an Hill, to be a Light to the World:

withal gradually shortening Man's Life, 'till it was brought to be but

about one twelfth Part of what it used to be before the Flood; And

so, according to Dr. T vastly cutting off, and diminishing his Tempta-

tions to Sin, and increasing his Excitements to Holiness. And now

let us consider what the Success of these Means was, both as to the

Gentile World, & the Nation of Israel.

Dr. T. justly observes (Key P. 33 (Key. §. 50.) "The Jewish dispensation

"had Respect to the Nations of the World, to spread the Knowledge &

"Obedience of God in the Earth; and was established for the Benefit

"of all Mankind."[^002-note9] But how unsuccessful were these Means, & all other

Means used with the heathen Nations, so long as this dispensation

lasted. Abraham was a Person noted in all the principal Nations

that were then in the World; as in Egypt, & the eastern Monarchies;

God made his Name famous by his wonderful distinguishing dispensa-

tions towards him, particularly by so miraculously subduing before

him, & his trained Servants those Armies of the four eastern Kings.

This great Work of the most high God, Possessour of Heaven & Earth,

was greatly taken Notice of by Melchizedeck; and one would think

should have been sufficient to have awaken'd the Attention & Con-

sideration of all the [-] Nations in that Part of the World, & to have

led them to the Knowledge & Worship of the only true God; especially

if considered in Conjunction with that miraculous & most terrible

destruction of Sodom, and all the Cities of the Plain for their Wick-

edness, with Lot's miraculous deliverance; which doubtless were

Facts, that in their Day, were much famed abroad in the World.

But there is not the least Appearance, in any Accounts we have, of

any considerable good Effect. On the contrary, those Nations which

were most in the Way of observing & being affected with these things,

even the Nations of Canaan, grew worse & worse, 'till their Iniqui-

ty came to the full in Joshua's Time. And the Posterity of Lot,

that Saint so wonderfully distinguished, soon became some of

the most gross Idolaters, as they appear to have been in Moses's

Time. (see Num. 25 XXV.) Yea and the far greater Part of even

of Abraham's Posterity, The Children of Ishmael, Ziman, Jokshan,

Medan, Midian, Ishback, & Shuah, & Esau soon forgot the true

God, & fell off to Heathenism.

[^002-note10]Great things were done in the Sight of the Nations of the World,

tending to awaken them, & lead them to the Knowledge and Obe-

dience of the true God, in Jacob's & Joseph's Time; in that God did

CHAP. I Against Great Means. [^002-note11]

SECT. VIII.

miraculously, preserve from perishing by the Hand of Joseph, preserve

from perishing by Famine, as it were the whole World; as appears by

Genesis 41. [-] 56, 57. Agreeably to which, the Name which Pharaoh

gave to Joseph, Zaphnath-Paaneah, as is said, in the Egyptian

Language, signifies Saviour of the World. But there does not

appear to any good abiding Effect of this; No not so much as in the

Nation of the Egyptians; (which seems to have been the chief of all the

heathen Nations at that Day) who had these great works of Jeho-

vah in their most immediate View: on the contrary, they grew worse

& worse, & seem to be far more gross in their Idolatries, & Ignorance

of the true God, & every way more wicked, & ripe for Ruin, when

Moses was sent to Pharaoh, than they were in Joseph's Time.

After this in Moses & Joshua's Time, the great God was pleased

to manifest Himself in a Series of the most astonishing Miracles,

for about fifty years together, wrought in the most publick Man-

ner, in Egypt, in the Wilderness & in Canaan, in the view as it

were of the whole World; Miracles by which the world was

shaken, the whole Frame of the visible Creation, Earth, seas &

Rivers, the Atmosphere, the Clouds, sun, Moon & stars were

affected; Miracles greatly tending to convince the Nations of the

World of the Vanity of their false God's, shewing JEHOVAH to be infinitely

above them, in the Thing wherein they dealt most proudly, and exhi-

biting God's awful Displeasure for the wickedness of the heathen

world. And these things as one End of these great Miracles,

As in Exodus 9:15. Numbers 14:21. Joshua 14:23-24. And other Places.

But no Reformation followed these Things, but by the Scripture Ac-

count, the Nations which had them most in View, were dreadfully har-

den'd, stupidly refusing all Conviction & Reformation, & obstinately went

on in an Opposition to the living God, to their own destruction.

After this, God did from time to time, very publickly manifest him-

self to the Nations of the World, by wonderful Works wrought in the

Time of the Judges, of a like Tendency with those already mention'd.

Particularly in so miraculously destroying by the Hand of Gideon, almost

the whole of that vast Army of the Midianites, Amalekites & all the

Children of the East, consisting of about 135000 Men Judges 7:12 &

Judges 8:12. But no Reformation followed either this or the other great

works of God wrought in the Times of deborah & Barak, Jephtha &

Sampson.

After these Things, God uses new, & in some Respects much greater

Means with the heathen world, to bring them to the Knowledge & ser-

vice of the true God, in the days of david & Solomon. He raised up

david a Man after his own Heart, a most fervent Worshipper of the true

God, & zealous Hater of Idols, & subdued before him all almost all

the Nations, between Egypt & Euphrates; often miraculously assisting

him in his Battles against his Enemies; and confirmed Solomon his

Son in the full & quiet Possession of that great Empire, for about forty

years; and made him the wisest, richest, most magnificent, & every

way the greatest Monarch that ever had been in the World; & by far

the most famous, & of greatest Name among the Nations; especially for

his Wisdom, & things concerning the Name of his God; Particularly the

Temple he built, which we are told was exceeding magnificent, that

it might be of Fame & Glory throughout all Lands 1 Chronicles 22:5. And

we are told, that there came of all People to hear the wisdom of Solo-

mon from all Kings of the Earth 1 Kings 4:34 & 1 Kings 10:24. And the Scrip-

ture informs us that these great things were done, that the Nations in

far Countreys might hear of God's great Name, & of his stretched out[^002-note12]

Arm

The Heathen World obstinate PART I. [^002-note13]

Arm; that all the People of the Earth might fear him, as well as his

People Israel; And that all the People of the Earth might know that

the LORD was God, and that there was none else 1 Kings 8:41-42,

1 Kings 8:43, 60. But th still there is no Appearance of any considerable abid-

ing Effect, with regard to any one heathen Nation.

After this, before the Captivity into Babilon, many great Things were

done in the Sight of the Gentile Nations, very much tending to enlight-

en, affect & perswade them. As God's destroying the Army of the

Ethiopians, of a Thousand Thousand, before Asa; Elijah's & Eli-

sha's Miracles; especially Elijah's miraculously confounding Baal's

Prophets & Worshippers, Elisha's healing Naaman, the King of

Syria's prime Minister, and the miraculous Victories obtain'd thro'

Elisha's Prayers over the Syrians, Moabites & Edomites, The miracu-

lous destruction of the vast united Army of the Children of Moab,

Ammon & Edom, at Jehoshaphat's Prayer 2 Chronicles 20. Jonah's

preaching at Nineveh, together with the Miracle of his delive-

rance from the Whales Belly; which was published & well at-

tested, as a Sign in to confirm his Preaching: But more especially

that great Work of God, in destroying Sennacherib's Army by an

Angel, for his Contempt of the God of Israel, as if he had been

no more than the Gods of the Heathen.

When all these Things proved ineffectual, God took a new Method

with the heathen World, & used, in some respects, much greater Means

to convince and reclaim them than ever before. In the first Place,

his People the Jews were removed to Babilon, the Head of the hea-

then Idolatry (Chaldea having been very much the Fountain of

Idolatry) to carry thither the Revelations which God had made

of Himself, contained in the sacred Writings; and there to bear

their Testimony against Idolatry; as some of them, particularly

Daniel, Shadrach, Meshach & Abednego b in a very open Man-

ner, before the King, & the greatest Men of the Empire, with such

Circumstances as made their Testimony very famous in the World;

God confirming it with great Miracles; which were published thro'

the Empire by Order of it's Monarch, as the mighty works of the

God of Israel, shewing him to be above all Gods: daniel, that great

Prophet, at the same time being exalted to the Governour of all

the Wise-men of Babilon, & one of the chief Officers of Nebu-

chadnezzar's Court.

After this, God raised up Cyrus to destroy Babilon for it's obstinate

Contempt of the true God, and Abuse of his Injuriousness towards

his People; According to the Prophecies of Isaiah, speaking of him

by Name, instructing him in the Vari [ ] concerning the Nature

& dominion of the true God, Isaiah 45. which Prophecies were

probably shown to him, whereby he was induced to publish his Tes-

timony concerning the God of Israel THE GOD. Ezra 1:2-3. Dan-

iel about the same Time, being advanced to be prime Minister

of State in the new Empire, erected under darius, And in that

Place appeared openly as a Worshipper of the God of Israel, & Him

alone; God confirming his Testimony for God before the King, &

& all the Grandees of his Kingdom, by preserving him in the den

of Lions; whereby darius was induced to publish to all People, Na-

tions & Languages, that dwelt in all the Earth, his Testimony that

the God of Israel was the living God & steadfast forever &c.

CHAP. I in their Wickedness [^002-note14]

SECT. VIII.

When after the Destruction of Babilon, some of the Jews returned to their

own Land, Multitudes never returned, but were dispersed abroad thro'

many Parts of the vast Persian Empire; as appears by the Book of Est-

her. And many of 'em afterwards, as good Histories inform, were re-

moved into the more western Parts of the World; and so were dis-

persed as it were all over the heathen World, having the holy Scripture

with them, & synagogues every where, for the Worship of the true God.

And so it continued to be till the Days of Christ & his Apostles; as

appears by the Acts of the Apostles. Thus that Light which God

had given them, was in the Providence of God carried abroad into

all Parts of the World: So that now they had far greater Advantages

to come to the Knowledge of the Truth in Matters of Religion, if they

had been disposed to improve their Advantages.

And besides all these things, from about Cyrus's Time, Learning

& Philosophy increased, and was carried to a great Height. God raised

up a Number of Men of prodigious Genius to instruct others, & im-

prove their Reason & Understanding in the Nature of things: And

philosophic Knowledge having gone on to increase for several Ages,

Seem'd to be come to its Height before Christ came, or about that

Time.

And now let it be considered what was the Effect of all these

Things. —— The Heathen World Instead of a Reformation, or any

Appearance or Prospect the Heathen World in general rather grew

worse. As dr. Winder observes, "The inveterate Absurdities of Pa-

gan Idolatry continued without Remedy, and increased as Arts &

"Learning increased; and prevailed in all Paganism prevailed in all

"its Height of Absurdity, when Pagan Nations were polished to the

"Height, and in the most polite Cities & Countreys; and thus conti-

"nued to the last Breath of Pagan Power."[^002-note15] And so it was with re-

spect to wickedness in general, as well as Idolatry, as appears by

what the Apostle Paul observes in Romans 1. Dr. T. speaking of the

Time when the Gospel Scheme was introduced (Key § 257.) Says

"The moral & religious State of the Heathen was very deplorable, being

"Generally Sunk into great Ignorance, gross Idolatry, & abominable Vices."[^002-note16]

Abominable Vices prevailed, not only among the common People, but

even among their Philosophers themselves, yea some of the chief

of them, of & of greatest Genius; as Dr. T. himself observes as to

that detestable Vice of Sodomy, which they commonly & openly al-

lowed & practiced without Shame. See dr. T—r's Note on Romans 1.

27.[^002-note17]

Having considered[^002-note18] the State of the Heathen World, with regard

to the Su Effect of means used was to p for it's Reformation, during

the Jewish dispensation, from the first Foundation of it in Abraham's

Time; Let us now consider how it was with that People themselves, that

were distinguished with the peculiar Priviledges of that dispensa-

tions. The Means used with the Heathen Nations were great; but

they were small compared[^002-note19] with those used with the Israelites.

The Advantages by which that People were distinguished are repre-

sented in represented in Scripture as vastly above all parallel, in

Passages which dr. T. takes notice of (Key. §. 39.)[^002-note20] And he reckons

these Priviledges among those which he calls antecedent Blessings,

consisting in Motives to Virtue and Obedience; And says (Key. §. 51.)

"That this was the very End & design of the dispensation of God's ex-

"traordinary Favours to the Jews viz. to engage them to duty & Obedience,

"or that it was a scheme for promoting Virtue, is clear beyond dispute,

The Obstinacy of the Jews PART I.[^002-note21]

"from every Part of the old Testament."[^002-note22] Nevertheless, as has been

already Shown the Generality of that People, thro' all the succes-

sive Periods of that dispensation, were Men of a wicked Cha-

racter . But it will be more abundantly manifest, how Strong

the natural Biass to Iniquity appeared to be among that People,

by considering more particularly how things were with them

from Time to Time.

Notwithstanding the great things God had done in the Time of Abra-

ham, Isaac & Jacob, to separate them, & their Posterity from the Idola-

trous World, that they might be a holy People to himself; yet in less than

200 Years about 200 Years after Jacob's Death, & in less than 150

Years after the Death of Joseph, which was while some were alive

that had seen Joseph, the People had in a great Measure lost the

true Religion, and were apace conforming to the Heathen World.

When for a Remedy, & the more effectually to alienate them from Idols,

& engage them to Himself, God appeared to bring 'em out from among

the Egyptians, and separate them from the heathen World, & to re-

veal Himself in his Glory & Majesty, in so affecting & astonishing a

Manner, as tended most deeply & durably to impress their Minds;

that they might never forsake him more. But so perverse were

they, that they murmured even in the Midst of the Miracles that

God wrought for 'em in Egypt, and murmured at the Red-Sea, in

a few Days after God had brought them out with such a mighty

Hand. When he had led them thro' the Sea, they sang his Praise,

but soon forgot his Works. Before they got to Mount Sinai they

openly manifested their Perverseness from Time to Time; so that

God says of 'em, Exodus 16:28. How long will ye refuse to keep my

Commandments & my Laws? Afterwards they murmured again

at Rephidim.

In about two Months after they came out of Egypt they came to

Mount Sinai; where God entered into a most solemn Covenant with

the People, that they should be an holy People unto him, with such

astonishing Manifestations of his Power, Majesty & Holiness, as were

altogether unparallel'd: As God puts the People in Mind Deuteronomy 4:32.

For ask now of the days that are past, which were before thee, Since the

day that God created Man upon the Earth; and ask from one Side of

Heaven unto the other, whether there has been any such thing as

this great Thing is, or hath been heard like it. Did ever People hear

the Voice of God speaking out of the Midst of the Fire, as thou hast

heard, and live? Or hath God assayed to take him a Nation from the

Midst of another Nation &c? And these great Things were to that

End, to impress their Minds with such a Conviction & Sense of divine

Truth, and their Obligations to their Duty, that they might never

forget them: As Gods says, Exodus 19:9. Lo, I come unto thee in a thick

Cloud, that the People may hear when I speak with thee, & believe

thee forever. But what was the Effect of all? Why it was not more

than two or three Months before that People, there, under that very

Mountain, returned to their old Egyptian Idolatry, and were singing

& dancing before a golden Calf which they had set up to worship.

And after such awful Manifestations as there were of God's displeasure

for that Sin, and so much done to bring 'em to Repentance, and confirm 'em

in Obedience, it was but a few Months before they came to that Vi-

olence of Spirit in open Rebellion against God, that with the ut-

most vehemence they declared their Resolution th to follow God no

longer, but to make them a Captain to return into Egypt. And thus

CHAP. I in their Wickedness. [^002-note23]

SECT. VIII.

they went on in ways of perverse opposition to the most high, from

time to time repeating their open Acts of Rebellion, for forty Years

'till that Generation was destroyed in the Midst of continued as-

tonishing Miracles, 'till that Generation was destroyed. And tho'

the following Generation seems to have been the best that ever was

in Israel; yet Moses says concerning them, Deuteronomy 29:2-4. Ye

have seen all that the Lord did before your Eyes &c. Yet the Lord hath

not given you an Heart to perceive, nor Eyes to see, nor Ears to hear

unto this Day. And Notwithstanding all the wonders of God's Pow

er & Love to that People in Joshua's Time, how soon did that People de-

generate, & begin to forsake God, & join with the Heathen in their Ido-

latries, 'till God by severe Means, & by sending Prophets & Judges, ex-

traordinarily influenced from above, reclaimed them? & brough But when

they were brought to some Reformation by such Means, they soon fell

away again into the Practice of Idolatry; and so from Time to

Time, from one Age to another; No & nothing proved effectual for

any abiding Reformation.

After things had gone on thus for several hundred Years, God used new

Methods with his People, in two Respects; First, He raised up a great

Prophet in Israel, under whom a Number of young Men were

trained up in Schools, that from among them there might be a constant

Succession of great Prophets in Israel, of such as God should chuse; which

Seems to have been continued for more than 500 Years. Secondly,

God raised up his servant David,[^002-note24] one eminent for Wisdom, Forti Piety &

Fortitude, to subdue all their heathen Nieghbours, who used to be such

a Snare to 'em; & to confirm, adorn & perfect the Institutions of his

publick Worship; And by him to make a more full Revelation of the

great Salvation, & future glorious Kingdom of the Messah. And af-

ter him raised up his Son Solomon, the wisest & greatest Prince that

ever was on Earth, more fully to settle andestablish those Things

which his Father David had begun, concerning the publick Worship of

God in Israel, and to build a glorious Temple for the Honour of Jehovah

JEHOVAH, and the Institutions of his Worship, and to instruct the

neighbour Nations in true Wisdom & Religion. But as to the Success of

these new & extraordinary Means, if we take dr. T. for our Exposi-

tor[-] of Scripture, the Nation must be extremely corrupt in david's

Time: He supposes he has respect to his own Times, in those words

Psalms 14:2-3. The Lord looked down from Heaven, to see if there were

any that understand, & seek God: They are all gone aside; They are

together become filthy; There is nonoe that doth Good, no not one. But

whether dr. T. be in the right in this or not, yet if we consider what

appeared in Israel in Absalom's & Sheba's Rebellions, we shall not see

Cause to think that the greater Part of the Nation at that day were Men

of true Wisdom & Piety. As to Solomon's Time Dr. T. supposes, as has been

already observed, that Solomon speaks of his own Times, when he says

he had found but one in a Thousand that was a thoroughly upright

Man. However it appears, that all those great means used to promote, &

establish Virtue, and true Religion in Samuel's, david's & Solomon's Time,

were so far from having any general abiding good Effect in Israel that

Solomon himself with all his Wisdom, and notwithstanding the unparallel'd

Favours of God to him had his Mind corrupted, so as openly to tolerate Idola-

try in the Land, & greatly to provoke God against him. And assoon as he was

dead, ten Tribes of the twelve forsook the true worship of God, & instead

of it establ openly established the like Idolatry, that the People fell into

at Mount Sinai, when they made the golden Calf, and continued [-]

finally

Obstinacy of the Jews PART. I. [^002-note25]

finally obstinate in this Apostacy, notwithstanding all Means that

could be used with them by the Prophets, which God sent, one after

another, to reprove, counsel & warn them, for about 250 Years; espe-

cially those two great Prophets, Elijah & Elisha. Of all the Kings that

reigned over them, there was not so much as one but what was of a

wicked Character. And at last it came to that, that their Case seem'd

utterly desperate: so that Nothing remain'd to be done with them, but to

remove 'em out of God's Sight. and cast them wholly away. Thus the Scrip-

ture represents the Matter 2 Kings 17.

And as to the other two Tribes; tho' their Kings were alwaies of

the Family of David, and they were favoured in many respects far

beyond their Brethren, yet they were generally exceeding corrupt: their

Kings were most of 'em wicked Men, and their other Magistrates, &

Priests & People were generally agreed in the Corruption. Thus the Matter

is represented in the Scripture History, & the Books of the Prophets. And

when they had seen how God had cast off the ten Tribes, instead of

taking warning, they made themselves more vile than ever the other

had done; as appears by 2 Kings 17:18-19. Ezekiel 16:46-47, 51.

God indeed waited longer upon them, for his Servant david's sake,

& for Jerusalem's Sake, that he had chosen; & used more extraordi-

nary Means with them; especially by those great Prophets Isaiah

& Jeremiah; but to no Effect: So that a last it came to that, as

the Prophets represent the Matter, that they were like a Body

universally & desperately diseased & corrupted, that would admit

of no Cure, the whole Head sick, & the whole Heart faint &c.

Things being come to this Pass, God took this Method with them:

He utterly destroyed their City & Land, & the Temple which he had

among them, made thorough Work in purging the Land of 'em;

as when a Man empties a dish & turns it upside down; or as a

Vessel cast into a fierce Fire, 'till it's Filthiness is thoroughly burnt

out; 2 Kings 21:13. Ezekiel 24. They were carried into Cap-

tivity, & there left 'till that wicked Generation was dead, & those

old Rebels were purged out; that afterwards the Land might be

resettled with a more pure Generation.

After the Return from the Captivity, & God had built the Jewish

Church again in their own Land, by a Series of wonderful Providences,

Yet they corrupted themselves again to so great a Degree, that

the Transgressours were come to the full again in the days of Anti-

ochus Epiphanes; as the Matter is represented in the Prophecy of

Daniel, Daniel 8:28. And then God made them the Subjects of

a dispensation, little, if any Thing, less terrible than that which had

been in Nebuchadnezzar's days. And after God had again deliver'd

'em, and restored the State of Religion among them, by the Instrumen-

tality of the Maccabees; They degenerated again; So that when

Christ came, they were so extremely corrupt, as Accounts given

by the Evangelists represent.[^002-note26]

It may be observed here in general, that the Jews, tho' so vastly dis-

tinguished with Advantages, Means & Motives to Holiness, yet are

represented as coming from Time to Time to that degree of Corrup-

tion & Guilt, that they were more wicked than the very worst of

the Heathen. So of old God Sware by his Life, that the wickedness

of Sodom was small compared with that of the Jews. Ezekiel 16:47,

Ezekiel 16:48 &c. also Chap. 5 V. 5 —— 10. So Christ speaking of the Jews in his

Time, represents 'em as farr more stubborn & perverse in wickedness,

& of and having much greater Guilt than the Inhabitants of Tyre

CHAP. I The Gospel resisted. [^002-note27]

SECT. VIII.

& Sidon, or Sodom & Gomorrah.[^002-note28]

But we are now come to the Time when the grandest Scene was displayed,

that ever was open'd on Earth. After all other Schemes had been so long, &

so thoroughly tried, and had so greatly faild of Success, both among Jews &

Gentiles; that wonderful dispensation was at Length introduced, which

was the greatest scheme for the suppressing & restraining Iniquity among

Mankind, that ever infinite Wisdom & Mercy contrived; even the glorious

Gospel of Jesus Christ. "A new dispensation of Grace was erected (to use

"dr. T—r's own words) for the more certain, & effectual Sanctification of

"Mankind into the Image of God, the delivering them from Sin & Wick-

edness, into which they might fall, or were already fallen, to redeem

"'em from all Iniquity, & bring 'em to the Knowledge & Obedience of God."[^002-note29]

In whatever high & exalted Terms the scripture speaks of the Means

& Motives which the Jews enjoyed of old; yet their Priviledges are re-

presented as having no Glory in Comparison of the P Advantages of the

Gospel. Dr. T. says (Key dr. T—r's words P in P. 233 are worthy to

be here repeated, "Even the Heathen (says he) knew God, and might

"have glorified him as God; but under the glorious Light of the Gospel,

"we have very clear Ideas of the divine Perfections, and particularly of the

"Love of God as our Father, and as the God & Father of our Lord & Savi-

"our Jesus Christ. we see our Duty in the utmost Extent, & the most

"cogent Reasons to perform it: we have Eternity open'd to us, even an

"endless State of Honour & Felicity, the Reward of Virtuous Actions;

"& the Spirit of God promised for our Directiion & Assistance. And all

"this may & ought to be applied to the purifying our Minds, & the per-

"fecting of Holiness. And to these happy Advantages, we are born:

"for which we are bound forever to praise & magnify the rich Grace of

"God in the Redeemer."[^002-note30] And he elsewhere says *[^002-note31] "The Gospel Constitu-

"tion, is a Scheme the most perfect & effectual for restoring true Re-

"ligion, & promoting Virtue & Happiness, that ever the World has yet seen"

And y[^002-note32] That it is admirably adapted to enlighten our Minds &

Sanctify our Hearts; & [-] **[^002-note33] that never were motives so divine

& powerful proposed to induce us to the Practice of all Virtue &

Goodness.

And yet even these Means have been ineffectual with the far greater

Part of them with whom they have been used: Of the many that have

been called few have been chosen.

As to the Jews, God's antient[^002-note34] People with whom they were used in the

first Place, & used long by Christ & his Apostles, the Generality of them

rejected Christ & his Gospel with extreme Pertinaciousness of spirit. They

not only went on still in that Career of Corruption which had been in-

creasing from the Time of the Maccabees; but Christ's Coming, & his doc-

trine & Miracles, and the Preaching of his Followers, & the glorious Things

that followed the same that attended the same, were the Occasion, thro'

their perverse Improvement, of an infinite Increase of their wickedness.

They crucified the Lord of Glory, with the utmost Malice & Cruelty, &

persecuted his Followers; they pleased not God, & were contrary to all

Men; and went on to grow worse & worse, 'till they fill'd up the Mea-

sure of their Sin, & Wrath came upon them to the uttermost; & they

were destroyed, & cast out of God's Sight, with unspeakably greater

Tokens of the divine Abhorrence & Indignation, than in the days of

Nebuchadnezzar

The Gospel generally PART I [^002-note35]

Nebuchadnezzar: The bigger Part of the whole Nation were slain, & the

rest were scatter'd abroad thro' the Earth, in the most abject & for-

lorn Circumstances. And in the same spirit of Unbelief & Malice

against Christ and the Gospel, and in their miserable dispersed

Circumstances do they remain to this Day.

And as to the Gentile Nations; tho' there was a glorious Success of the

Gospel amongst them in the Apostles days; yet probably not one in ten

of those that had the Gospel preach'd to 'em embraced it. The Power of

the World was set against it, and persecuted it with insatiable Ma-

lignity. And among the Professours of Christianity, there presently ap-

pear'd in many a Disposition to Corruption, & to abuse the Gospel

to the Service of Pride and Licentiousness. And the Apostles in their

days foretold a grand Apostacy of the Christian World, which should

continue many Ages; and observed that there appeared a disposition

to such an Apostacy among professing Christians, even in that day,

2 Thessalonians 2:7. And the greater Part of the Ages which have now

elapsed, have been spent in the Duration of that grand & general

Apostacy, under which the Christian World, as it is called, has been

transformed into that, which has been vastly more deformed, more

dishonourable and hateful to God, & repugnant to true Virtue,

than the prece foregoing State of the heathen World before. Which

is agreeable to the prophetical descriptions given of it by the ho

Holy Spirit.

In these latter Ages of the Christian Church God raised up a Num-

ber of great & good Men, to bear Testimony against the Corruptions

of the Church of Rome, and by their Means introduced that Light

into the World, by which, in a short Time, at least one Third of

Europe were delivered from the more gross Enormities of Antichrist;

which was attended at first with a great Reformation, as to vital

& practical Religion. But how is the Gold soon become dim! To

what a Pass are things come in Protestant Countreys at this Day,

& in our Nation in particular! To what a prodigious Height has a

deluge of Infidelity, Profaneness, Luxury, debauchery & wickedness

of every Kind arisen! So that perhaps no Age can afford The poor

savage Americans are meer Babes [-] and Fools (If I may so speak) as

to Proficiency in Wickedness, in Comparison of with Multitudes that

the Christian World throngs with. dr. T. himself, as was before

observed, speaks of rep represents that the Generality of Christians

have been the most wicked, lewd, bloody & treacherous of all

Mankind; And says, (Key § 356) "The Wickedness of the Christian

"World renders it so much like the Heathen, that the good Effects

"of our Change to Christianity are but little seen."[^002-note36]

And with respect to the dreadful Corruption of the present day, is

to be considered, th besides the Advantages already mention'd, the great

Advances in Learning and Philosophic Knowledge, which have been

made in the present & past Centuryes, giving great Advantage for

the a proper & enlarged Exercise of our rational Powers, & for the seeing

the bright Manifestation of God's Perfections in his Works. And it

is to be observed, that the Means & Inducements to Virtue which this

Age enjoys, are in Addition to most of those which were mention'd

before as given of old; Particularly in Addition And among other

Things, in Addition to the shortening of Mans' Life to 70 or 80

years, [-] from near a 1000 Thousand. And with regard to this, I

would observe, that as the Case now is in Christendom, take one with

another of them that ever come to Years of discretion, their Life is

not[^002-note37]

And all this may and ought to be applied to the purifying our Minds, and the perfecting of Holiness. To this Light, and to these happy Advantages we are born; "for which we are bound for ever to praise and magnify the rich Grace of "God in the Redeemer."

## Fragment I.I OS 157-179

Jonathan EdwardsFragment I of *Original Sin*

Possibly part of a penultimate or at least a late draft of Edwards' defense of the doctrine of original sin, this manuscript fragment corresponds to the last third or so of the printed treatise, beginning on p. 250, part-way through Pt. II, Ch. IV, sect. I, and continuing to the end. While the basic form of the finished product is here, there are significant differences between this draft and the printed text, which nicely illustrate Edwards' process of composition.[^003-note1]

***

The manuscript is an infolded folio quire of eighteen sheets (Maid of Dordt/GRwr) stitched at the fold. Edwards numbered the pages 157-224. There are also several smaller pieces—salvaged letter covers and other scraps—either stitched or pinned in, on which Edwards wrote insertions cued to neighboring pages. The last leaf, which is missing the top third, is unnumbered and blank, and probably served as a cover.

Fragment I of *Original Sin*

[^003-note1]: See WJE 3:315437; for MS examples, see *ibid*., 88-92.

[^003-note2]: **marginal.** [157]

[^003-note3]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, pp. 216-17, on Romans 1:32: "[Into this vicious State the Generality of the Heathens were sunk;] and although they were not ignorant of the Rule of Right, which God has implanted in the Human Nature, (and which shews, that they who commit such Crimes are deserving of Death) yet, as a further Aggravation of their Wickedness, they not only commit these Things themselves, but are pleased with, and encourage others in the Practice of them."

[^003-note4]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 233, on Romans 4:15: "Because Law, or a Rule of Duty, makes no Provision for the Exercise of Mercy, or for Trust in that Mercy, but leaves the Sinner, and would have left *Abraham*, as he was an Idolater, exposed to Punishment without Hope."

[^003-note5]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, pp. 77-78: "But it may very justly be further observed, that there is still a great bar in the Way of Life, *viz*. the SIN in which the World is involved. Sin in its own unalterable Nature leadeth to Death."

[^003-note6]: **marginal.** [158]

[^003-note7]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 38: "Death came upon Mankind, not for their personal Sins, but upon account of *Adam*'s one Transgression: That it was his first Sin alone his one Offence, which subjected Mankind to Death." P. 40: "The whole of the Apostle's Argument and Assertion evidently stands upon these two Principles; that it is by the ONE OFFENCE of *Adam* that Death passed upon all Men, and not by their own personal Sins: And again, that it is by the OBEDIENCE OF ONE, or the one Act of *Christ*'s Obedience . . . that all Men are justified unto Life, and not by their own personal Righteousness." P. 42*n*: "Tho' Men were Sinners during the Period between *Adam* and *Moses*, yet they were not subjected to Death, or made mortal by their Transgressions . . . But yet Men through that long Tract were all subject to Death; therefore they must be included in the Sentence, Genesis 3:19. and their Mortality must be the Consequence of *Adam*'s one Offence." Pp. 53-54: "[S]eeing the Apostle's whole Argument turns upon this Point, That all Men die, not thro' their *own* Sins, but thro' the one Offence of *Adam*, who can doubt but the Words, *for that all have sinned* [Romans 4:12], must be understood in a like Sense to those, *all are made Sinners*, however the particular manner of expression be accounted for?" Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 393: "This demonstrates, that no Man, in this World, is under Law, the Covenant of Works, or the broken Law of Works. For if we were *now*, at any Time, under the broken Law of Works, then should we be in a State of final and eternal Damnation, without Hope, or Remedy: Because there now remains *NO* *MORE* Sacrifice for Sins, Hebrews 10:26. But it is one first and grand Principle of the Gospel, that we are not under *Law*, but under Grace, Romans 6:14."

[^003-note8]: MS sic; "won't"? The first edition reads: "But it follows from this Doctrine."

[^003-note9]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 148: "If you say, *this is owing to the Mercy of God in the Redeemer, which restoreth us to what we lost in Adam*: I answer, The Scripture . . . saith, that all we lost in *Adam* was that Life which ceaseth when we leave this World."

[^003-note10]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 361: "The Consequences of *Christ*'s *OBEDIENCE* extend as far as the Consequences of *Adam*'s *DISOBEDIENCE*: But those extend to all Mankind; and therefore so do the Consequences of *Christ*'s Obedience. . . . It was the *Law*, which for *Adam*'s one Transgression, subjected him, and his Posterity, as included in him when he transgressed, to Death without Hopes of a Revival. 'Tis *GRACE*, or the Favour of the Lawgiver, which restores all Men to Life at the Resurrection."

[^003-note11]: For Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 361, see above, n. 9. *Original Sin*, pp. 39-40: "Something in the *Free-gift* in *Christ* answereth to something in the *Offence* of *Adam*. . . . Though now I intend to draw a comparison betwixt them, yet you ought to know, that the Grace and Benefits in the Redeemer OVERFLOW and ABOUND far beyond the Consequences, the ill Effects of the first Offence. . . . *Therefore* or *wherefore*, these Things being thus explained, *As by the offence of one*(Adam) *judgment came upon all men to condemnation, even so by the righteousness of one, the free gift came upon all men to justification of life.*"

[^003-note12]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 364-65: "For both the Grace, which takes off the Consequence of *Adam*'s Offence, and the Grace which abounds beyond it, are both included in the same Carisma, or *Free-Gift*. Which should be well observed: For in this I conceive lies the Connection, and Sinews of his Argument. The *Free-Gift*, which stands opposed to *Adam*'s *Offence*, and which, I think, was bestowed immediately after his Offence, (Gen. iii. 15. *The Seed of the Woman shall bruise the Serpent's Head*;) this *Gift*, I say, includes both the Grace, which answers exactly to the Offence, and also that Part of the Grace which stretches far beyond it. And if the one Part of the *Gift* be freely bestowed upon all Mankind, as the *Jews* allow, why not the other?" P. 366: "The same Thing indeed may also be said of the *Death*, which we all now die, considered as *abolished* by the Grace of God in *Christ,*, introducing a Resurrection. *Thus* considered, it is not contrary to Grace, but consistent with it; and therefore is not removed by the Grace of the Gospel: For Death still reigns so as to bring all Men to the Grave. But the Apostle, when he considers Death as the Consequent of *Adam*'s Sin, considers it as such; that is, he considers it *antecedently* to the Grace of God in *Christ*." In p. 395, commenting on Romans 7:8, Taylor states that the Jews were not under the Law before it was given at Mt. Sinai, and therefore not under the sentence of death. "Which was not the Case of the rest of Mankind; who then were, (as they had always been from the Time of that Promise,Genesis 3:15,) under the general Covenant of Grace, given first to *Adam*, and afterwards to *Noah*."

[^003-note13]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 148: "So that as we could not justly, by *Adam*'s Sin, lose Communion with God, it is true, in Fact, that we have not lost it."

[^003-note14]: This tipped-in leaf is made of a salvaged letter addressed on verso to JE.

[^003-note15]: **marginal.** [-] that Comes [159]

[^003-note16]: **marginal.** [160]

[^003-note17]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 40: "Certain it is therefore that the Apostle draweth a Comparison between the *Disobedience of Adam*, by which [. . .] *the many i.e.* all Men, are brought under Condemnation, and the *Obedience of Christ*, by which all Men are *justified unto Life*." P. 47: "Again observe, that the *Justification to* LIFE, [. . .] is such a Justification as comes upon ALL MEN, just as *the* DEATH, which answereth to it in the Comparison, [. . .] is said to *pass,* or come upon ALL MEN." P. 49: From all this it followeth, That as *the judgment, that passed upon all men to condemnation*, is Death's coming upon all Men, by the judicial Act of God, upon occasion of *Adam*'s Transgression: so *the free Gift's coming upon all men, to Justification of life*, is the reversing of that Condemnation to Death; or the appointing them to Life again after they are dead, by a like judicial Act of God, upon the account of *Christ*'s Righteousness." P. 60*n*: "Thus; *As through one*OFFENCE *upon* ALL MEN *unto* CONDEMNATION *of Death:*so*through one*RIGHTEOUS ACT *upon* ALL MEN *unto* JUSTIFICATION *of Life*." P. 61: "For since upon the Account of one Man's Lapse, the Sentence of Condemnation extended unto all Men; it must be true and fit, that the revoking that Condemnation, by the righteous Action of one, should likewise extend to all Men, to deliver them from the Mortality to which they were adjudged, and to restore them to Life at the Resurrection." P. 62: "For as, upon the account of one Man's Disobedience, Mankind were judicially constituted Sinners, *i.e. subjected to Death* by the Sentence of God, the Judge: So it is proportionably right and true, that, by the Obedience of one, Mankind shall be judicially constituted righteous, by being raised to Life again."

[^003-note18]: **marginal.** [161]

[^003-note19]: Taylor, *Paraphrase,* pp. 360-61: "Here [Romans 5:12 ff.] the Apostle advances his third and last Argument, to prove the Extensiveness of the Divine Grace, or that it reaches to all Mankind as well as to the *Jews*. Which Argument stands thus. The Consequences of *Christ*'s *OBEDIENCE* extend as far as the Consequences of *Adam*'s *DISOBEDIENCE*: But those extend to all Mankind; and therefore so do the consequences of *Christ*'s Obedience." On p. 362, Taylor argues that Paul's argument from the beginning of Romans has been "the Extensiveness of the Grace of the Gospel, that it actually reaches to *ALL* *MEN*, and is not confined to the *Jewish* Peculiarity."

[^003-note20]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 367: "It is no Objection to this Sense; that the *Grace of God* is here said to have abounded to *the many*, that is, to all Mankind: for this means no more than, what the Apostle constantly affirms, That it is free to all." P. 362, where Taylor states that the Apostle "lays down that Position in a diversified Manner of Speech, *V*. 18, 19, [. . .] and leaves us to conclude, from the Premises laid down, *V*. 15, 16, 17, that the Gift and Grace, or Favour, of God in its utmost Extent, is as free to all Mankind, who are willing to accept of it, as this particular Instance, the Resurrection from the Dead."

[^003-note21]: **marginal.** [162]

[^003-note22]: I.e. Romans 12:5.

[^003-note23]: **marginal.** [163]

[^003-note24]: Philip Doddridge, *The family expositor: or, a paraphrase and version of the New Testament* (6 vols. London, 1739-56), 4:378*n*, on 1 Corinthians 15:1, "unworthy hope": "It is well known, that the primitive *Christians* were often insulted by the *Heathen Philosophers*, for their Hope of a Resurrection; which one of them, ridiculously enough, calls *the Hope of Worms*." Compare 2 Timothy 11:18. and the *Note* there. Others taught Virtue to be *its own necessary* Reward, in such a Manner, as tended to verthrow the strongest of all natural Arguments for a future State; I mean, that taken from such an unequal Distribution of Rewards and Punishments, as could not otherwise take Place under the Government of a righteous GOD. If *Christians* were by this tempted so to refine on the Doctrine of the *Resurrection*, as in Effect to explain it away, it shews the Propriety of the *Apostle's* setting himself to prove the Resurrection of *Christians*, rather than a Resurrection in general."

[^003-note25]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 408: "But what have the Wicked to do with Immortality, who shall *be punished with everlasting*DESTRUCTION, 2 Thessalonians 1:9? *Immortality, Incorruptibility, [. . .]*are never attributed to the Wicked in Scripture, but always to the Righteous alone."

[^003-note26]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 402: "The Revival, or Resurrection of the Body, is frequently put for our Advancement to eternal Life."

[^003-note30]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 54*n*, considering Romans 5:12: "For in the 12th verse he considers the Entrance of Death into the World, in a *general* Way, and no further than it is the Consequence of the Sin of *one Man*. But when he is entered fully into his Argument, he saith, *all are made Sinners*, which, besides the Sin of *Adam*, includeth this further Thought, that we are in a State of suffering, or subject to Death, by the Sentence and judicial Act of the Lawgiver."

And the *Greek* of the Septuagint Version, which was commonly read by those *Jews* who lived in foreign Countries, and spake the *Greek* Language, will serve to shew us what Words in the *Hebrew* correspond to the *Greek* Words which the Apostles use. For the Apostles use the *Hellenistic Greek*, into which the Old Testament is translated, and which the *Jews* in their Dispersions commonly read."

[^003-note47]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 33, on Romans 5:19: "But let it be remembred, the Apostle is not here *quoting* any Text out of the Old Testament; and therefore may well be supposed to express himself in his own Way: Which whether it doth not as fully and truly convey the Idea of the *Hebrew* Word, as any Word or Words the *Septuagint* have chosen, the Learned will easily judge."

[^003-note48]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 136, on Job 31:28 and Isaiah 44:1: "We easily see the Sense in these two last Places; and what hinders but we may as clearly see the same Sense in a like Phrase in the Text under Consideration [Psalms 51:5]? Shall we indulge unreasonable Prejudices in the Face of the brightest Scripture-evidence?"

[^003-note49]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 178, on Colossians 3:10 and Ephesians 4:24: "For, Ephesians 2:10, *We are God's workmanship, created in Christ Jesus*, by the Dispensation of Wisdom and Grace in Christ, *unto good works*. CHAP. II. The Evidence of the doctrine

of Original sin from what the SS. teaches concerning the Application

of Redemption.

The Truth of the doctrine of original sin is most clearly manifest

from the Representations which the SS. scriptures make of that Change of

state which they speak from what the SS. says of that Change of state which

it represents as necessary to in order to an Interest in the spiritual & Eternal

Blessings of the Redeemers Kingdom

In order to this They speak of it as absolutely necessary for every one that [^004-note18]

he be regenerated or born again as John 3:3. verily ver[il]y I say unto

thee except a man be born again He cannot see the Kingdom of God.

The dr. T- tho he will not allow that this signifies any Change from a

state of sin natural Propensity to sin yet supposes the New Birth here spo-

ken of means a mans being brought to a spiritual & divine Life in a

right Use & application of the Natural Powers in a Life of true Holiness

(p. 144) And that it is to [ ] the Attainmt of the Habits those Habits

of Vertue & Religion which give us the real Character of true Xtians & the

Children of God . (p. 246) 248. a[)] & that it is putting on the new nature

of right Action & true Holiness . (p. 251. c.[)][^004-note19]

But in Order to proceed in the most safe & sure manner in our End

understanding what is meant by being born again in SS. & in the Inferences

we draw from it Let us compare SS. with SS. & consider what is said of the ne-

cessity of such our being the subjects of such a Change that Change of state which

is thereby represented Let us compare SS. with SS. and consider what other Terms

are used & or Phrases are used in other Places where the SS. has evidently Re-

spect to the same Change. And here I would observe

1. It is manifest that by Regeneration or being begotten or born again is

mean the same Change in a mans the state of the mind of is meant

which is brought to pass in & expressed in Repentance & Conversion Re-

the word me metanoia most commonly used for Repentance signifies a Change

of the mind and as the word Conversion signifies a turning or Turning

plainly means a turning of the mind from sin to God and doubtless Re-

pentance & Conversion mean much the same thing Change or turning

And if we compare one SS. with another it will be plain that this Change

is the very same with that which is called Regeneration or at least the former

is the active that there is no real but more than a relative differ & no

real difference as the wo in the words Reg Regeneration may be signified this

Change as the mind may be passive [in][^004-note20] it & in the words Conversion & Repentance

[-]th may signify the same as the mind is active in it. As in In that

attaind that constitutes the Character of a true Xtian and is necessary to for th

the Kingdom The same Benefit [-] in like manner express'd is in like man-

ner suspended on Conversion as in the other Place on Regeneration.

jections

CHAP. I. Concerning that Objection that to suppose

that men are born in sin without their own Choice or any previous Act of their

own will is ag inconsistent with the Nature of Sin

Some of the Objections that are made against the doctrine of original

Sin that have Reference to particular Arguments used in defence of it having al-

ready been considered under the Head of those Arguments I shall now

the Chief Objections that remain.

Their Objection which dr. against the doctrine of mans being born in sin

which he insists upon above on m above all others is taken from the Pe-

Pelagian and Arminian Notion of the Freedom of the Will consisting in the

the Wills self-determination as necessary to the Being of moral good or

Evil vertue or sin. He often insists upon that if sin we come into the ¤

infected with sinful & depraved dispositions then sin must be natural to us & if na-

tural then necessary & if necessary then no sin & that w it cannot be to

blame for it & be in any Respect our Fault being what we cant hep help that

sin must proceed from our own Choice [margin. see p. 125, 128, 129, 130. a. b

186, 187, 188 e. 190. e. 200. a, b. 245, 246, 253, 25 a 258 c. 339, 340,

437. & other Places.][^004-note32]

Here I would observe in general this Not The Notion which of late has

of the Forementioned Notion of the Freedom of Will as Essential to moral

Agency and necessary to the very Existence of moral Good or Evil seems to

a main Point in all almost controversies betw with Pelagians and Arminians

& all that are infected with their Enemies in their Controversies with those

that used to be called Orthodox there is no one thing more Fundamental

in their schemes of Religion And in the determination of that one Point

depends the Issue of almost all the Controversies we have with this sort

of divines . Nevertheless tis needless for me particularly to consider this Point

matter in this Place Having already very largely considered it with all the

main Grounds of it & Arguments used to defend it to which I must refer

the Reader . Tis very necessary that that matter the modern prevailing doc

trine about th this matter should be thorough well understood & the

thoroughly considered & examined there is no Hope of putting an End

to this Controversy about original sin & innumerable other Controversies

that subsist about many of the main d Points of Religion I stand ready to

Confess that if the Pelagians & others that follow em in their notion of

our modern that if they can maintain their doc this their Notion

of free will consisting in self the self determining Power of the will as

necessary to moral Agency & thoroughly establish it against the Arguments

that lie against I stand ready to yield it to em that they have an impregna-

ble Castle which from which it is in vain for Calvinists to to which they

may repair & remain un invincible in all the Controversies they have with

the Calvinists concerning the doc of original sin, Election Efficacious

Grace Redemption, Conversion & Efficacious Grace The Nature

of saving Faith & concerning Perseverance in Holiness and other things

gain'd an Advantage And at the same Time I think their doctrine

CHAP. II. Concerning that Objection against the doctrine

of Native Corruption. That to suppose that to suppose that first receive

their first Existence in sin is to suppose that Him that is the Author of their Being

their depravity The Author of de their depravity

Another Objection which dr. T greatly insists upon it against the doctrine

of mens being born with sinful depravity of Nature . which dr. T. greatly

insists upon is that this doc does in effect Charge him who is the author of

our Nature & that has formed us in the Womb with being the Author

of the dep of a sinful Corruption of Nature And that this is highly in

jurious to the God of our nature whose Hands have fashioned & formed us

to believe our Nature is originally corrupted & in the worst sense of corrup-

tion. [p. 137, 187, 188, 189, 256, 258, 260, 419, 424, & following Pages

& other Places.][^004-note43]

With Respect to this Objection so much urged Argument so much urged

by the doctor I would in the First Place Observe that He in his Handling

this Argument supposes somethings as belonging to the doctrine as maintaind

by such as are called orthodox divines that do [-] not belonged to it

nor are in the lest supposed in it or Consequent upon it As particularly

The doctrine of original sin to imply that nature must be corrupted by

some positive Influence something by some means or other infused

into the human Nature some Quality or other not from the Choice of our

own Minds but like a Taint Tincture or Infection altering the Natural

Constitution Faculties & disposition of our souls" so p 187. b. "That

sin is implanted sin & evil dispositions are implanted in the Fœtus

in the womb [p. 146. c. & [-] the like in many other Places]

whereas truly our supposing that men are corrup born depraved & corrupt

with a moral depravity yea totally corrupt implies no such thing as

any positive Influence Quality disp or disposition Taint or Infection

implanted or infused or or wrought in the nature of man by any

positive Influence whatsoever either any positive act of divine

Power or any positive Influence of Parents or of [ ] any Posi-

tive operation of the Laws & Course of nature or any other other

C positive Cause Or that that The doctrine of original de-

pravity yea & of mans being born with a Corruption yea a total

Corruption of Nature will not imply nor infer any such Thing or

that nor is there the least need of supposing in order to account

for a total native depravity of the Heart of man of supposing any

Positive Cause of that Corruption & or that it arises man is con-

ceived and born with any positive fountain of Evil in his Heart

Heart from whence it springs I think a little Attention to the

nature of Things will be sufficient to satisfy any impartial Considerate

that the main absence of positive Good Principles & so the meer

witholding of a divine Influence to impart & uphold

those good Principles & leaving nature to it is a leaving those natural [^004-note44]

Principles of self-love natural appetites to &c to themselves without

the government of divine Principles is sufficient to render the Heart of

man Corrupt yea totally corrupt without any positive Influence

Cause or Influence at all And that this is apparently the Case with

Respect to the corruption of Nature that came on Adam himself imme-

diately on his Fall & that comes on all his Posterity on as fall sin-

ning in Him & falling with Him.

The Case with man with Regard to the Corruption & Ruin of his Nature

when he fell was plainly This . When God made man at first he

made him with implanted in him two different Kinds of Principles

ples He gave him There were spiritual & divine Principles

CHAP. III. That Grand Objection against the Imputation

of Adams sin to his Posterity Considered that to suppose such an

Imputation is unju is unjust & unreasonable inasmuch as Adam

& his Posterity are not the same with a Considerat some brief Reflexion on

It would take up too much Room here to repeat

That we may proceed with he greater Clearness in this matter I would

first observe some things for the right Stating and more distinct con-

ceiving of the doctrine of the Imputation of Adams first sin & then

then secondly say something in Opposition to the great Clamour made a-

gainst the Reasonableness of the doctrine

First I would observe some things for the right stating and more

clear conceiving of of this doctrine and the more clear & distinct

conceiving of this Affair of the Imputation of Adams sin.

And I think it would go far towards directing us to proper & true

Notions of the matter if we steadily bear this in mind that God in

his Proceedings with Adam in this affair looked on his Posterity as one

with him (as to the propriety of his looking upon them so I shall speak after-

wards) And that in those he dealt with the whole species or Race

Adam & all his Posterity by Posterity by Ordinary Generation together as one

he dealt more immediately w with the Root Adam yet it was as the

Root of the whole Tree & Head of the whole Body ye yet he

dealt with all the Branches & all in their Root & all with all the

their Head & Root. And that

also upon them just as it did upon Him as much as if they all coexisted

Alterations throughout the whole tree in every individual Branch

[^004-note4]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 111: "He [Paul] well understood the Worth of the human Nature; and, in other Places, shews it was endowed, even in the *Gentiles*, with Light and Powers sufficient to have known God, and performed Obedience to his Will." P. 63: "For no Man can be obliged to do, nor can any reasonable Being expect he should do what he has not sufficient Power to do." P. 64: "we may very safely and assuredly conclude, that Mankind in all Parts of the World have sufficient Powers to do that Duty which God requires of them, and that he requires of them no more than what they have sufficient Powers to do."

[^004-note5]: A tipped-in, quarto-sized letter cover, addressed on recto, "To the Revd / Mr / Jonathan Edwards / Pastor of ye Chh / of Christ / In / Northampton / pr Mr / Lyman."

[^004-note6]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 228: "Therefore to give a direct and final Answer to the Objection taken from Romans 7. we may from thence gather, that we are very apt, in a World full of Temptation, to be deceived and drawn into Sin by bodily Appetites; that when once we are under the Government of those Appetites, it is at least exceeding difficult, if not impracticable, to recover ourselves by the mere Force of Reason; and consequently that we stand in need of that Life-giving Spirit." *Paraphrase*, pp. 247-52, on Romans 7, and p. 253, on Romans 8. P. 253: "Thus under the weak and lifeless Dispensation of the *Law*, the Sinner remains in a deplorable State, without Help or Hope, inslaved to Sin, and Sentenced to Death.".

[^004-note7]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 128: "For if our Sins proceed from his [Adam], his Sin is the Cause of ours; and the Cause of every Effect is alone chargeable with the Effect it produceth, or which proceedeth from it."

[^004-note8]: **marginal.** [rest of page blank]

[^004-note9]: **marginal.** [183]

[^004-note10]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, *Supplement*, p. 71 [348], where Taylor accuses Watts of establishing "unthankfulness" as "a supposed Principle of the Christian Religion." He continues, "Divines maintain, that as soon as we receive our Existence we are under the Wrath, Curse, and heavy Displeasure of God; and then the *softest* Inference (which in itself is a very high Degree of Ungodliness) is, that we cannot thank him for our Existence. But if we cannot reasonably thank God for our Entrance into Life, or Existence, which is the fundamental Blessing, without which we can receive no other, then we cannot reasonably thank him for any Thing besides.—And why can't we reasonably thank God for our Existence? Partly *because the Bulk of Mankind are born to Trouble and Misery*."

[^004-note11]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 201: "And as for *Law*, or the Rule of right Action, it was absurd for any Part of Mankind to expect Pardon, or any Blessedness upon the Foot of that, seeing all Mankind had broken it."

[^004-note12]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 379, taking up immediately after passage quoted in n. 9: "Certainly the Law was not brought in among the *Jews* to be a Rule of Justification; or to recover them out of a State of Death, and to procure them Life by their sinless Obedience to it: For in this, as well as in another Respect it *was weak*, not in itself, but through the Weakness of our *Flesh*, Romans 8:3. The Law, I conceive, is not a Dispensation suitable to the Infirmity of the Human Nature, in our present State."

[^004-note13]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, *Supplement*, p. 92 [368]: "But his [Adam's] Posterity have been, and still are, under the mild Dispensation of GRACE, which makes Allowance for our Infirmities, provides Succour under Temptations, vouchsafes the Benefit of Repentance, and promises the Remission of Sin."

[^004-note14]: I.e. MS p. 182a.

[^004-note15]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 44: "He [God] hath not only taken off those Consequences [of Adam's sin], but over and above hath conferred a rich Overplus of Grace, in erecting a new Dispensation, furnished with a glorious Fund of Light and Truth, Means and Motives." P. 50: "Here, by the Way, we may see a good Reason why the Scripture speaks so sparingly of the Consequences of *Adam*'s Sin upon us: Because as these are freely absolved and reversed to Mankind in *Christ*, so we are not so much concerned to know them, as to understand, and improve the ABOUNDING Grace, the Means and Season of Grace which God hath *abundantly* provided in a Redeemer, for the cultivating of our Nature."

[^004-note16]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 224: "All that we can learn from the Faults of good Men in Scripture is, that they are obnoxious to Temptation, and may be overcome if they are negligent and secure: And further, that, through the Mercy of 'God, it is possible he who hath sinned may see the Error of his Way, and return to the Obedience of the Just." *Supplement*, pp. 68 ff. [342 ff.] discusses the parable of the talents: "EVERY ONE *of us shall give Account of*HIMSELF, and of no other, *to God*, Romans 14:12. Everyone shall be judged according to the particular Powers and Talents God has given *him*, and not according to the Powers and Talents which God originally gave to *Adam*" (p. 68 [324]). Pp. 60-61 [337-38]: "[Man] is born under a Hazard of living in Sin, and perishing for ever.—True; but this results from the very Nature of a State of Probation, which places Man under a Possibility of sinning and of being destroy'd. But this is no Fault of his Nature, nor any Defect in the Favour of his Maker. If he lives in Sin, 'tis his own foolish and wicked Choice." P. 234: "All Men in the World are born to *some Light*, and *some Advantages*, for which they are accountable; tho' only according to the several Degrees of their Light and Advantages."

[^004-note17]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, pp. 182-83: "The Wickedness of the *Christian* World, which renders it so much like that of the *Heathen*, that the good Effects of our Change to Christianity, or of our being the People and Children of God, are but little seen; and therefore the Grace, which grants us the Privileges and Blessings we abuse, is but little regarded and valued." On p. 321, Taylor agrees to an objection that "*great number in the Christian World have been, at least, as vicious as the worst of the Heathen ever were*," and states that the "Corruptions, which still remain in the Christian World, require a new and further Dispensation to reform them."

P. 248: ""Now, it is most evident without an habitual Subjection of the Will to Reason and Truth, or to the Will of God, none can be fit to be Members of this Society or Kingdom." [^004-note28]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, *Supplement*, pp. 150-55 [425-29], discusses the "old man" and the "new man," as mentioned in Ephesians 4:22-24. Taylor asserts this distinction does not refer "to our Fall in *Adam*, nor to any Corruption of Nature derived from him, but to their *Gentile* State, and wicked Course of Life, from which they [the ancient Ephesians] were lately converted to Christianity" (152). "From all this I apprehend we may gather, that the *Old Man* relates to the *Gentile State*, and that the *New Man* is either the *Christian State*, or the Christian Church, Body, or Society" (154).

[^004-note29]: Taylor, pp. 224: "Christians, too generally neglecting the Study of Scripture, content themselves with a few Scraps, which, though wrong understood, they make the Test of Truth, and the Ground of their Principles, in Contradiction to the whole Tenor of Revelation."

[^004-note32]: Taylor, *Original Sin,*p. 125, answering the proposition, "That *Sin must be natural to us*," used to support imputed sin: "And if *natural*, then *necessary*, with regard to *us*; and if necessary, with regard to us, then *no Sin*, with regard to us, or so far as WE are concerned in it." P. 129: "Again, Upon these Principles, all actual Transgression, or all the Sin that hath been, is, or shall be in the World, proceedeth from *Adam*'s Sin, either by necessary Consequence [. . .] or through the Choice of our Wills, or partly by the one, and partly by the other. If by necessary Consequence in the whole, or in part, then all the Sin that hath been, is, or shall be in the World, is no Sin at all, either in the whole, or in part: for what proceedeth necessarily, so far as it proceedeth necessarily, can be no Sin." On pp. 186-90, Taylor refutes the objection that we "*derive from Adam a moral*Taint*and*Infection*, whereby we have a natural Propensity to Sin*," arguing that this ultimately makes God the author of sin. Taylor makes virtually the same point on necessity, in similar language, in the other pages cited by JE.

[^004-note33]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 128: "For if our Sins proceed from his [Adam], his Sin is the Cause of ours; and the Cause of every Effect is alone chargeable with the Effect it produceth, or which proceedeth from it."

[^004-note34]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 190: "But by *Propagation* it is not *possible* Parents should communicate Vice; which is always the faulty Choice of a Person's own Will, otherwise it is not a Vice." P. 200: "The Apostle, (*Rom*. chap. vii.) doth not speak of himself, or of any other Man, as he cometh into the World, but as he is afterwards depraved and corrupted by his own wicked Choice." P. 216, on Rom. 7:15: "That the Sin which the Sinner commits, is what he doth not allow with his Understanding and Reason: for what his Reason approves and dictates, that he doth not; but what he hateth, what is abhorrent from his Reason, that he doth, wickedly choosing what his own Conscience tells him is false and odious."

[^004-note35]: **marginal.** [193]

[^004-note36]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 232: "His [Adam's] Sin was not from a depraved Nature, but from his own disobedient Will: And so *must* every Man's Sin, and all Sin in the World, how much soever, be, as well as his."

[^004-note37]: Taylor, *Original Sin, Supplement,* p. 166 [442]: "I might add, that, upon the Scheme we are examining, *Adam*'s Inclination to Sin (for he could not sin without a sinful Inclination) must be so strong in him, as to overcome a natural in-bred Propensity to Holiness."

[^004-note38]: Taylor, *Paraphrase*, p. 388: "Commenting upon the 5th Verse he [Paul] shews [. . .] That the Law reached to all the Branches and latent Principles of Sin, V. 7."

[^004-note39]: Taylor, *Original Sin*, p. 125: "Therefore Sin is not natural to us, and therefore I shall not scruple to say, this Proposition in the *Assemblies Catechism* is false."
